\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\rho\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\tfrac{1-2\alpha}{2}}\left\lVert\nabla\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\tfrac{1+2\alpha}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\kappa_{1}\left\lVert\rho\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\Bigg)^{\tfrac{1-2\alpha}{4}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\sigma\kappa_{1}\left\lVert\nabla\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\Bigg)^{\tfrac{1+2\alpha}{4}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\kappa_{1}^{-1}\sigma^{-\frac{1+2\alpha}{2}}\Bigg)^{1/2};

\diamond

If
\alpha\in[1/2,1)
, we have
\check{\alpha}\in(0,3/2-\alpha)
and whence

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{3/(1-\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\nabla\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2(1-\alpha)}\left\lVert\nabla\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2\alpha-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\sigma^{\check{\alpha}}\kappa_{1}\left\lVert\nabla\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\Bigg)^{1-\alpha}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}+\check{\alpha}}\kappa_{1}\left\lVert\nabla\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\Bigg)^{\alpha-\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\kappa_{1}^{-1}\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}-(\alpha+\check{\alpha})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

The final step is devoted to obtaining an
L^{1}((0,t),L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
estimate for
\nabla u
and
G(\rho,c)
, with
p\in[6/\alpha,\infty)
.

To achieve this, we follow the computations that lead to (
3.97
) and we obtain

p\dfrac{d}{dt}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}H_{p-1}(\rho,c)\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}+\dfrac{\rho_{*}g_{*}}{\nu^{*}}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}H_{p-1}(\rho,c)\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{p}}\leqslant\dfrac{1}{\nu_{*}}\Bigg(\frac{2\rho^{*}g^{*}}{a_{p,*}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

and time integration, together with (
3.95
), yields:

k_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert G(\rho,c)\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C_{*}C_{0}+k_{p}^{*}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

It follows that (using again (
3.63
)–(
A.8
)):

\displaystyle k_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert G(\rho,c),\,\mu_{*}\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}C_{0}+C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\left\lVert\rho\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{3p/(3+p)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\check{\phi}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{6p/(1+p\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\big(k_{*}+{k_{p}^{*}}\big)\sup_{[0,t]}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho,c)\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Under the smallness assumption on the shear-viscosity jump

\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\Bigg(1+\frac{k_{p}^{*}}{k_{*}}\Bigg)\sup_{[0,t]}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho,c)\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\leqslant\frac{1}{2},

the last term can be absorbed into the LHS, and Young’s inequality gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert G(\rho,c),\,\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C_{*}C_{0}+C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\left\lVert\rho\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{3p/(3+p)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\check{\phi}_{\Sigma}^{\frac{3}{\alpha}\big(1-\frac{2}{p}\big)}\left\lVert\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}C_{0}\Bigg(1+\check{\phi}_{\Sigma}^{\frac{3}{\alpha}\big(1-\frac{2}{p}\big)}\Bigg)+C_{*}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\kappa_{1}\left\lVert\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{3}{2p}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\sigma\kappa_{1}\left\lVert\nabla\dot{u}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3}{2p}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\kappa^{-1}_{1}\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{3}{p}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
2.4
.
∎

4.
Proof of the main result

This section is devoted to the final step in the proof of the main result, Theorem
1.2
. It is divided into three steps. First, (see Section
4.1
) we construct an approximate sequence
(c^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
for the Cauchy problem (
2.1
). This sequence comes with the a priori estimates summarized in Section
2
. In Section
4.2
, we close these estimates using a bootstrap argument.

4.1.
Construction of approximate solutions

Usually, the approximate sequence
(c^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})
is constructed as the solution of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
) with initial data
(c_{0}^{n},\rho_{0}^{n},u_{0}^{n})
obtained by mollifying
(c_{0},\rho_{0},u_{0})
with a smooth kernel.
This approach is unsuited here, because smoothing the initial data would remove the density discontinuity. Furthermore, classical arguments
à la Feireisl-Novotný-Petzeltová
, or
à la P.–L. Lions
fail in the density-dependent
viscosity case; in particular, the well-known effective flux and vorticity degenerate completely. Motivated by this, we establish in
[
44
]
a local-in-time existence of weak solutions to the system (
2.1
) within a functional framework tailored to our setting.
In particular, beyond the initial data assumptions (
1.12
)-(
1.13
)-(
1.18
), it is enough to require
(\rho\dot{u})_{|t=0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
to construct the approximate sequence
(c^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})
to (
2.1
). In other terms, we shall consider the Cauchy problem:

(4.1)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}c^{n}+u^{n}\cdot\nabla c^{n}=0,\\
\partial_{t}\rho^{n}+\dvg(\rho^{n}u^{n})=0,\\
\partial_{t}(\rho^{n}u^{n})+\dvg(\rho^{n}u^{n}\otimes u^{n})+\nabla P(\rho^{n},c^{n})=\dvg\big(2\mu(\rho^{n},c^{n})\mathbb{D}u^{n}\big)+\nabla\big(\lambda(\rho^{n},c^{n})\dvg u^{n}\big),\\
(c^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})_{|t=0}=(c_{0},\rho_{0},u_{0}^{n}),\end{cases}

with
u_{0}^{n}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, fulfilling the so-called compatibility condition

(\rho^{n}\dot{u}^{n})_{|t=0}=\dvg\big(2\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}u_{0}^{n}+\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg u_{0}^{n}-P(\rho_{0},c_{0})+\widetilde{P}\big)I_{3}\big)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

We construct the sequence of initial velocity
(u_{0}^{n})_{n}
as follows.

Lemma 4.1
.

There exists a sequence
(u_{0}^{n})_{n}
of initial velocities such that

\dvg\big(2\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}u_{0}^{n}\big)+\nabla\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg u_{0}^{n}\big)-\nabla P(\rho_{0},c_{0})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3});

and

(4.2)

\displaystyle\lVert u_{0}^{n}-u_{0}\rVert_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}+\lVert u_{0}^{n}-u_{0}\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

We provide the proof of Lemma
4.1
below. With the sequence
(u_{0}^{n})_{n}
in place, we construct
(c^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})_{n}
by applying the Theorem
A.1
. This theorem refines our recent work in
[
44
]
, where local-in-time well-posedness for (
4.1
) was obtained assuming

\left\lVert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\ll 1.

Indeed, here we only require smallness of the shear viscosity
jump
. In particular, when the shear viscosity is continuous, the condition holds trivially. Thus, our result aligns with the well-posedness theory for smooth data.

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

Following Section 3.5 of
[
43
]
, we consider a sequence of
nonnegative and smooth mollifier
(\omega_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, and we denote by
\Pi_{0}
the stress tensor at the initial time

\Pi_{0}=2\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}u_{0}+\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg u_{0}-P(\rho_{0},c_{0})+\widetilde{P}\big)I_{3}.

We now consider the elliptic equation

(4.3)

-\dvg\Big(2\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}u_{0}^{n}+\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg u^{n}_{0}-P(\rho_{0},c_{0})+\widetilde{P}\big)I_{3}\Big)+d_{n}u_{0}^{n}=-\dvg\big(\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0}\big),

where
(d_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of positive real numbers is a sequence of positive real numbers to be specified later.

For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the existence and uniqueness of
u_{0}^{n}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
follow from the Lax–Milgram theorem. We are now left to prove (
4.2
). We add

\dvg\big(\Pi_{0}\big)
to both sides of (
4.3
)

(4.4)

\displaystyle-\dvg\big(2\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}\big(u_{0}^{n}-u_{0}\big)\big)-\nabla\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg\big(u_{0}^{n}-u_{0}\big)\big)+d_{n}u^{n}_{0}=-\dvg\big(\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0}-\Pi_{0}\big),

and basic energy estimate yields

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(2\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\lvert\mathbb{D}(u_{0}^{n}-u_{0})\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle+\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\lvert\dvg(u_{0}^{n}-u_{0})\rvert^{2}\big)(x)dx+d_{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\lvert u_{0}^{n}\rvert^{2}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big(\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0}-\Pi_{0}\big)(x)\mathbb{D}(u_{0}^{n}-u_{0})(x)dx+d_{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u_{0}^{n}(x)u_{0}(x)dx.

Applying Young’s and Korn’s inequalities, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big[\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\lvert\mathbb{D}(u_{0}^{n}-u_{0})\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle+\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\big(\dvg(u_{0}^{n}-u_{0})\big)^{2}\big](x)dx+\frac{d_{n}}{2}\lVert u_{0}^{n}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(4.5)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0}-\Pi_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}+\frac{d_{n}}{2}\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2},

and therefore

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lVert\nabla u_{0}^{n}-\nabla u_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=0\;\text{ and }\;\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\lVert u_{0}^{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

as soon as
(d_{n})_{n}
satisfies

(4.6)

\displaystyle d_{n}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0\;\text{ and }\;d_{n}^{-1}\left\lVert\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0}-\Pi_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

As a consequence,
u_{0}^{n}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}u_{0}
strongly in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. It now remains to prove
\lVert u_{0}^{n}-u_{0}\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0
.

To achieve this, we set
v_{0}^{n}=u_{0}^{n}-u_{0}
, and we rewrite (
4.4
) as

\displaystyle-\widetilde{\mu}\Delta v_{0}^{n}-\big(\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda}\big)\nabla\dvg v_{0}^{n}+d_{n}v_{0}^{n}

\displaystyle=-d_{n}u_{0}-\dvg\big(\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0}-\Pi_{0}\big)

\displaystyle+\dvg\big(2\big(\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\big)\mathbb{D}v^{n}_{0}\big)+\nabla\big(\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\lambda}\big)\dvg v^{n}_{0}\big)=:h_{0}^{n}.

From this, we deduce (recall that
\mathbb{P}
denotes the Leray projector)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}v_{0}^{n}=\mathscr{Z}_{n}*\mathbb{P}h_{0}^{n},\;\text{ and }\;\big(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P}\big)v_{0}^{n}=\check{\mathscr{Z}}_{n}*\big(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P}\big)h_{0}^{n};

where

\big(\mathscr{F}\mathscr{Z}_{n}\big)(\xi)=\frac{1}{d_{n}+\widetilde{\mu}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}},\;\text{ and }\;\big(\mathscr{F}\check{\mathscr{Z}}_{n}\big)(\xi)=\frac{1}{d_{n}+(2\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda})\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}.

For any
a>0
, we have

\frac{1}{1+a\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t(1+a\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2})}dt,

and whence

\check{\mathscr{K}}_{a}(x):=\mathscr{F}^{-1}\Big(\xi\mapsto\frac{1}{1+a\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}\Big)(x)=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\mathscr{F}^{-1}\Big(\xi\mapsto e^{-at\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}\Big)dt=a^{-3/2}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\mathscr{K}_{t}\Big(\frac{x}{\sqrt{a}}\Big)dt,

where
(\mathscr{K}_{t})_{t>0}
denotes the heat kernel. As a result, for all
q\in[1,\infty]
and
p\in[1,3/2)
, we have

\left\lVert\check{\mathscr{K}}_{a}\right\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant Ca^{-\frac{3}{2}(1-1/q)},\;\text{ and }\;\left\lVert\nabla\check{\mathscr{K}}_{a}\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant Ca^{-2+3/(2p)}.

Since
\mathscr{Z}_{n}={d_{n}}^{-1}\check{\mathscr{K}}_{\widetilde{\mu}/d_{n}}
,
it is straightforward to derive the following bound (for some
p\in(1,p_{0})
, and
1/\check{p}+1/p=1/2+1/p_{0}
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathscr{Z}_{n}*\dvg\mathbb{P}\big(2\big(\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\big)\mathbb{D}v^{n}_{0}\big)\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\mathscr{Z}_{n}*\mathbb{P}\nabla\big(\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\lambda}\big)\dvg v^{n}_{0}\big)\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lVert\nabla\mathscr{Z}_{n}\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lVert\nabla v_{0}^{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lVert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu},\,\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\lambda}\rVert_{L^{\check{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}d_{n}^{1-\frac{3}{2p}}\lVert\nabla v_{0}^{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\lVert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu},\,\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\lambda}\rVert_{L^{\check{p}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Hence, from (
4.6
)–(
4.5
), we obtain

(4.7)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathscr{Z}_{n}*\dvg\mathbb{P}\big(2\big(\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\big)\mathbb{D}v^{n}_{0}\big)\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\mathscr{Z}_{n}*\mathbb{P}\nabla\big(\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\lambda}\big)\dvg v^{n}_{0}\big)\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

Next, for
R>0
we write

\displaystyle d_{n}\mathscr{Z}_{n}*\mathbb{P}u_{0}

\displaystyle=\check{\mathscr{K}}_{\widetilde{\mu}/d_{n}}*\mathbb{P}u_{0}

\displaystyle=\check{\mathscr{K}}_{\widetilde{\mu}/d_{n}}*\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbb{1}_{B_{R}}u_{0}\big)+\check{\mathscr{K}}_{\widetilde{\mu}/d_{n}}*\mathbb{P}\big(\mathbb{1}_{B_{R}^{c}}u_{0}\big),

and it follows that

\left\lVert d_{n}\mathscr{Z}_{n}*\mathbb{P}u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C_{*}\Big(d_{n}^{\frac{3}{2}(1/p-1/p_{0})}R^{3(1/p-1/p_{0})}\lVert u_{0}\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\lVert\mathbb{1}_{B_{R}^{c}}u_{0}\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big).

Choosing
R=d_{n}^{-\frac{1}{4}}
, it is immediate that the first term converges to zero as
n\to\infty
; the same holds for the second term by the dominated convergence theorem.
In the following will now prove

(4.8)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathscr{Z}_{n}*\dvg\mathbb{P}\big(\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0}-\Pi_{0}\big)\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

Following the computations that led to (
4.7
), we obtain (
4.8
)
provided that

d_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0}-\Pi_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

However, this would require assuming
\Pi_{0}\in L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and hence
\nabla u_{0}\in L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
which we aim to avoid. To this end, we express

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}

\displaystyle=2\big(\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\mu}\big)\mathbb{D}u_{0}+\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})-\widetilde{\lambda}\big)\dvg u_{0}+\widetilde{\mu}\Delta u_{0}+\big(2\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda}\big)\nabla\dvg u_{0}-P(\rho_{0},c_{0})+\widetilde{P}

\displaystyle=:\Pi_{0,1}+\widetilde{\mu}\Delta u_{0}+\big(2\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda}\big)\nabla\dvg u_{0},

and assuming that

d_{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lVert\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0,1}-\Pi_{0,1}\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\xrightarrow{n\to 0}0,\;\text{ we readily obtain }\;\left\lVert\mathscr{Z}_{n}*\dvg\mathbb{P}\big(\omega_{n}*\Pi_{0,1}-\Pi_{0,1}\big)\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

Then we write the remaining term as

-\widetilde{\mu}\mathscr{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\xi\mapsto\frac{\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}{d_{n}+\widetilde{\mu}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}\mathscr{F}\mathbb{P}\big(\omega_{n}*u_{0}-u_{0}\big)\Bigg)-\big(2\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda}\big)\mathscr{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\xi\mapsto\frac{\xi}{d_{n}+\widetilde{\mu}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}\xi\cdot\mathscr{F}\mathbb{P}\big(\omega_{n}*u_{0}-u_{0}\big)\Bigg),

and a Fourier multiplier theorem (see e.g. Theorem 6.2.7 of
[
22
]
) yields

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widetilde{\mu}\mathscr{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\xi\mapsto\frac{\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}{d_{n}+\widetilde{\mu}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}\mathscr{F}\mathbb{P}\big(\omega_{n}*u_{0}-u_{0}\big)\Bigg)+\big(2\widetilde{\mu}+\widetilde{\lambda}\big)\mathscr{F}^{-1}\Bigg(\xi\mapsto\frac{\xi}{d_{n}+\widetilde{\mu}\left\lvert\xi\right\rvert^{2}}\xi\cdot\mathscr{F}\mathbb{P}\big(\omega_{n}*u_{0}-u_{0}\big)\Bigg)\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\left\lVert\omega_{n}*u_{0}-u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

Putting everything together, we obtain

\lVert\mathbb{P}\big(u_{0}^{n}-u_{0}\big)\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

Following the same computations, we also get

\lVert\big(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P}\big)\big(u_{0}^{n}-u_{0}\big)\rVert_{L^{p_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

This completes the proof of Lemma
4.1
. In addition, from (
4.4
) one readily deduces the strong convergence

\dvg\big(2\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\mathbb{D}u_{0}^{n}\big)+\nabla\big(\lambda(\rho_{0},c_{0})\dvg u_{0}^{n}\big)-\nabla P(\rho_{0},c_{0})\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}\dvg\big(\Pi_{0}\big),

in
\dot{H}^{-\check{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, as soon as
\dvg\big(\Pi_{0}\big)\in\dot{H}^{-\check{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.
∎

4.2.
The bootstrap argument and final steps

This section is devoted to closing all the a priori estimates derived in Section
2
. Since the initial density
is bounded away from zero and, in view of the assumptions on the shear viscosity coefficient
\mu=\mu(\rho,c)
,
we have the following bounds

0<\rho_{0,*}=\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho_{0}\leqslant\sup_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho_{0}=\rho^{*}_{0}<\infty;

\displaystyle 0<\mu_{0,*}:=\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\leqslant\sup_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})=\mu^{*}_{0}<\infty.

Let us define

\displaystyle L_{0}=\left\lvert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\Sigma_{0}\right\rVert_{\text{Lip}}+\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\left\lvert\nabla\varphi_{0}\right\rvert_{\text{inf}}^{-1}+\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\text{inf}}^{-1},

and let us consider the constants
\varepsilon_{1}
, and
\varepsilon_{2}
given in Theorem
A.1
below, for
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha/2
. It is obvious that
there exists

\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\mu_{0,*},\mu^{*}_{0},L_{0})>0
such that if

\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\leqslant\delta_{0},

then

\frac{1}{\mu_{0,*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{10},\quad\text{and}\quad\Psi\big(\Sigma_{0},\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0}),\nu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\big)\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon_{2}}{10}.

As a consequence, Theorem
A.1
ensures the existence of a unique solution
(c^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})
to (
4.1
), defined on a maximal time interval
[0,T_{n})
. On this time interval, the solution has
enough regularity such that the computations performed in the Section
2
-
3
make sense. We now
consider the functionals
\vartheta
,
\check{\vartheta}
,
\delta
,
\mathcal{A}_{1}
,
\mathcal{A}_{2}
and
\mathcal{A}_{3}
,
with
(c,\rho,u)
replaced by
(c^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})
. Let
T\in(0,T_{n})
be such that, for every
t\in[0,T]
we have

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle 0<\frac{\rho_{0,*}}{10}\leqslant\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{n}(t,x)\leqslant\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{n}(t,x)\leqslant 10\rho_{0}^{*},\\
\vartheta(t)\leqslant L_{1},\quad\check{\vartheta}(t)\leqslant L_{2},\quad\delta(t)\leqslant 10\delta_{0},\quad\mathcal{A}_{1}(t)\leqslant L_{3};\end{cases}

where
L_{1}
,
L_{2}
, and
L_{3}
will be fixed later. On the time interval
[0,T]
the density and the viscosity are lower and upper bounded as in (
2.7
). In particular, whenever
\delta_{0}
,
E_{0}
are below thresholds depending only
on the upper and lower bounds of density (and viscosity), Lemma
2.3
ensures that

\mathcal{A}_{1}(t)\leqslant C_{*}\Big(C_{0}\big(1+\check{\phi}_{\Sigma_{n}}^{\frac{6}{\alpha}}E_{0}\big)+\left\lVert\nabla u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}+E^{\frac{1}{4}}_{0}\big(\mathcal{A}_{1}(t)^{\frac{5}{4}}+C_{0}^{\frac{5}{4}}\big)\Big),

so that we can choose

L_{3}=5C_{*}\Big(C_{0}\big(1+\check{\phi}_{\Sigma_{n}}^{\frac{6}{\alpha}}E_{0}\big)+\left\lVert\nabla u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\Big)

and deduce that on the time intervale
[0,T]
, we have

\mathcal{A}_{1}(t)\leqslant\frac{3}{5}L_{3}.

Recalling the expression of
\check{\phi}_{\Sigma_{n}}
from (
2.10
), it follows that

\check{\phi}_{\Sigma_{n}}\leqslant C_{*}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(1+\delta_{0}K_{0}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}\big),

and we shall assume that

C_{*}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(1+\delta_{0}K_{0}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}\big)\big(C_{0}+\left\lVert\nabla u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\big)^{\beta}\leqslant C,\quad\text{ for some }\beta\in(0,\alpha).

This implies that

\check{\phi}_{\Sigma_{n}}^{\frac{6}{\alpha}}\big(C_{0}+\left\lVert\nabla u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{2}\big)\leqslant C,

and Lemma
2.4
provides us with

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\left\lVert\nabla u^{n},\,G(\rho^{n},c^{n})\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\dot{u}^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{6/(2-\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)\leqslant C_{*}L_{3}^{1/2}\big(1+L_{3}\big),\;\text{ and }\;\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\rho^{n}\dot{u}^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{3/(1-\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C_{*}\check{L}_{3},

where
\check{L}_{3}
depends nonlinearly on
\rho_{*,0}
,
\rho_{0}^{*}
,
\check{C}_{0}
.

Let us denote by
\varrho^{n}
the density
\rho^{n}
along the flow map
\mathcal{X}^{n}
of
u^{n}
. It is straightforward to deduce from the mass equation (
4.1
) that:

\partial_{t}\log\big(\varrho^{n}/\widetilde{\rho}(c_{0})\big)+\frac{1}{\nu(\varrho^{n},c_{0})}\frac{P(\varrho^{n},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}}{\log\big(\varrho^{n}/\widetilde{\rho}(c_{0})\big)}\log\big(\varrho^{n}/\widetilde{\rho}(c_{0})\big)=-\frac{1}{\nu(\varrho^{n},c_{0})}F^{n}\circ\mathcal{X}^{n},

and hence

\log\big(\varrho^{n}/\widetilde{\rho}(c_{0})\big)=e^{-\int_{0}^{t}g^{n}(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}}\log\big(\rho_{0}/\widetilde{\rho}(c_{0})\big)-\int_{0}^{t}\frac{e^{-\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}g^{n}(t^{\prime\prime})dt^{\prime\prime}}}{\nu(\varrho^{n}(t^{\prime}),c_{0})}(F^{n}\circ\mathcal{X}^{n})(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime},

where

g^{n}=\frac{1}{\nu(\varrho^{n},c_{0})}\frac{P(\varrho^{n},c_{0})-\widetilde{P}}{\log\big(\varrho^{n}/\widetilde{\rho}(c_{0})\big)}.

It follows that

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{n}(t,x)\leqslant\rho_{0}^{*}e^{C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert F^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},\;\text{ and }\;\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{n}(t,x)\geqslant\rho_{0,*}e^{-C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert F^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}},

where the
L^{1}((0,t),L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
norm of
F^{n}
is estimated as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert F^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\left\lVert\rho^{n}\dot{u}^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\rho^{n}\dot{u}^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{6/(2-\alpha)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\phi_{\Sigma_{n}}\left\lVert\nabla u^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big)

\displaystyle+C_{*}\sup_{[0,t]}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho^{n},c^{n})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{n})}\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert\nabla u^{n}\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha/2}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{n}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

From the expression of
\phi_{\Sigma_{n}}
given (
2.10
), we obtain

\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert F^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C_{*}L_{3}^{1/2}\big(1+K_{0}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}L_{2}^{1/2}\big)\big(1+L_{3}\big)+C_{*}\delta_{0}L_{1}^{1/2}\big(L_{1}^{1/2}+L_{2}^{1/2}\big).

We assume that
C_{0}
,
\left\lVert\nabla u_{0}\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}
and
\delta_{0}
are small so that the RHS above is less than
2\log(3)
. This implies

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{n}(t,x)\leqslant 9\rho_{0}^{*},\;\text{ and }\;\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho^{n}(t,x)\geqslant\frac{\rho_{0,*}}{9}.

Under the assumption that

C_{*}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\big(\delta_{0}^{1/2}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}+L_{2}^{1/2}\big)\leqslant 1,

(
2.1
) holds and and the bounds (
2.12
)–(

⋄

‣
2.1
) follow. In particular, one can choose

L_{2}=3C_{*}\big(\check{\vartheta}(0)+\check{L}_{3}\big)e^{C_{*}K_{0}(L_{1}+L_{3}^{1/2}(1+L_{3}))e^{CL_{1}}}.

What remains is to close the estimates for
\vartheta
and
\delta
. Recall that

\displaystyle\vartheta(t)

\displaystyle\leqslant e^{C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\lVert w^{n}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}}\Bigg\{C_{*}\left\lvert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rvert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha/2}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C_{*}L_{3}^{1/2}\big(1+L_{3}\big)\big(1+L_{2}\big)+C_{*}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\big(1+\delta_{0}^{1/2}L_{2}\big)K_{0}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}L_{3}^{1/2}\big(1+L_{3}\big)

\displaystyle+C_{*}\delta_{0}^{1/2}L_{2}^{1/2}\big(L_{2}+\delta_{0}^{1/2}L_{1}\big(1+K_{0}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}\big)\big)+C_{*}\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\ell_{\Sigma_{n}}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\left\lVert\llbracket G(\rho^{n},c^{n})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{p}(\Sigma_{n})}+\left\lvert\llbracket G(\rho^{n},c^{n})\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha/2}(\Sigma_{n})}\Big]\Bigg\},

with

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,t]}\left\lVert f(\rho^{n},c^{n})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left\lVert w^{n}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}\Bigg(\left\lVert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\big(1+L_{2}^{1/2}\big(1+K_{0}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}\big)\big)L_{3}^{1/2}\big(1+L_{3}\big)+\delta_{0}L^{1/2}_{1}\big(L^{1/2}_{1}+L^{1/2}_{2}\big)\Bigg).

From the rough bounds (
2.14
)–(
3.18
) for the pressure jumps, we derive

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\ell_{\Sigma_{n}}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\left\lVert\llbracket G(\rho^{n},c^{n})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{p}(\Sigma_{n})}+\left\lvert\llbracket G(\rho^{n},c^{n})\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha/2}(\Sigma_{n})}\Big]

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{*}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}\Bigg(L_{2}^{1/2}K_{0}\left\lVert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lvert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha/2}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+L_{3}^{1/2}\big(1+L_{3}\big)\big(1+L_{2}+K_{0}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}\big)

(4.10)

\displaystyle+\big(1+L_{2}^{1/2}\big(1+K_{0}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}\big)\big)L_{3}^{1/2}\big(1+L_{3}\big)+\delta_{0}^{1/2}L^{1/2}_{1}L_{2}^{1/2}\big(L^{1/2}_{1}+K_{0}\delta_{0}^{1/2}\big(L_{1}e^{C^{\prime}L_{1}}+L^{1/2}_{2}\big)\big)\Bigg).

One can first choose

L_{1}=\Big[10^{5}C_{*}\Big(\big(\check{\vartheta}(0)+\check{L}_{3}\big)^{1/2}K_{0}\left\lVert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lvert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha/2}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+L_{3}^{1/4}\Big)\Big]e^{C_{*}\big(1+L_{3}^{1/2}+\left\lVert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big)},

and then
L_{3}
and
\delta_{0}
small enough so that

\vartheta(t)\leqslant\Big(10^{-4}L_{1}\Big)e^{C_{*}K_{0}(L_{1}^{1/2}+L_{3}^{1/2}(1+L_{3}))e^{CL_{1}}}.

Therefore, under the smallness assumption on
\left\lVert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha/2}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}
and
L_{3}
, we have

\vartheta(t)\leqslant\frac{9}{10}L_{1}.

Observe that when the constitutive coefficients satisfy (
1.6
) or (
1.9
), we obtain sharper bounds (see Lemma
2.2
) for the pressure jump, which we shall use instead of (
4.10
). This sharper bounds allow us to
require smallness only on
\delta_{0}
and
L_{3}
, rather than
\left\lVert f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha/2}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}
. Furthermore, one readily proves that

\delta(t)\leqslant 9\delta_{0},

assuming that
\lVert\llbracket f(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha/2}(\Sigma_{0})}
(and also
\lVert\llbracket\mu(c_{0})\rrbracket\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha/2}(\Sigma_{0})}=\text{cnst}
, if (
1.6
) holds) is small enough. If neither (
1.6
) nor (
1.9
) holds, we should
make use of the rough bound (
4.2
) and deduce the above conclusion assuming that
L_{1}
and
L_{3}
are small. A continuation argument yields
T=T_{n}
.

To complete Theorem
1.2
, we shall show that
T_{n}=\infty
using the continuation criterion (see item 3 of Theorem
A.1
).
The convergence of the sequence
(c^{n},\rho^{n},u^{n})_{n}
to a solution
(c,\rho,u)
of (
1.1
) follows by arguments
analogous those of
[
43
, Section 3.2]
. The solution
(c,\rho,u)
has the regularity stated in Theorem
1.2
. In particular the velocity
u
is Lipschitz, which allows a change of variables to Lagrangian coordinates and, by adapting Proposition 3.1 of the above reference, yields the uniqueness of
(c,\rho,u)
. Let us observe that we will need
\sigma\nabla u\in L^{2}_{\text{loc}}([0,\infty),L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
. This improved time integrability follows by repeating the computations leading to (

⋄

‣
2.1
).
This proves Theorem
1.2
.

Appendix A
Local-in-time well-posedness

This section aims to outline how the smallness condition on the shear viscosity fluctuation in Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.5 of
[
44
]
can be relaxed to a smallness condition on the viscosity jump, as stated in Theorem
A.1
below.

Theorem A.1
.

Consider the system (
1.1
) with initial data
(c_{0},\rho_{0},u_{0})
satisfying (
1.12
)-(
1.13
)-(
1.18
)-(
2.2
), and let
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha)
. There exist positive constants

\varepsilon_{1}
, and
\varepsilon_{2}
depending only on
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
such that if

(A.1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\leqslant\varepsilon_{1},

and
2
2

2

Recall that
\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma}
is a polynomial in
\left\lvert\Sigma\right\rvert
,
\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\text{Lip}}
, and
\left\lvert\Sigma\right\rvert_{\text{inf}}^{-1}
.

\displaystyle\Psi\big(\Sigma_{0},\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0}),\nu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\big)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})}\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{0})}+\big(\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha^{\prime}}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Bigg(\frac{1}{\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})}\left\lvert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0}),\,\nu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\Bigg(\frac{1}{\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})}\Bigg)^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\frac{1}{\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0})}\left\lvert\mu(\rho_{0},c_{0}),\,\nu(\rho_{0},c_{0})\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\ell^{-\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\Bigg]^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}}\Bigg\}

(A.2)

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{2},

then there exist a time
T>0
and a unique solution
(c,\rho,u)
satisfying the following properties.

(1)

Energy bounds
:

\displaystyle u\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})),\;\,\dot{u}\in\mathscr{C}([0,T],L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}));

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sigma}\nabla\dot{u},\sigma\ddot{u}\in L^{\infty}((0,T),L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}));

\displaystyle\nabla\dot{u},\sqrt{\sigma}\ddot{u},\sigma\nabla\dot{u}\in L^{2}((0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}).

(2)

Piecewise Hölder bounds
: There exists
r_{\alpha}\in(0,8/3)
such that

\sigma^{r_{\alpha}/4}\nabla u\in L^{4}((0,T);L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})).

As a consequence, the velocity field
u
admits a flow map
\mathscr{X}
that transports the initial domain
D_{0}
to
D(t)=\mathscr{X}(t)D_{0}
, and
c(t)=\mathbb{1}_{D(t)}
,
t\in[0,T]
. Moreover, we have: for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\rho(t)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\partial D(t)}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\;\text{ and }\;\int_{0}^{T}\sigma^{r_{\alpha}}\left\lVert\nabla u(t^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\partial D(t^{\prime})}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{4}dt^{\prime}<\infty.

(3)

Continuation criterion
: Let
T^{*}
be the lifespan of
(c,\rho,u)
. If
T^{*}<\infty
, then we have either

\limsup_{t\to T^{*}}\Bigg(\left\lVert\rho(t),\,\frac{1}{\rho(t)},\frac{1}{\mu(\rho(t),c(t))}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\rho(t)\right\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\partial D(t)}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\nabla u(t),\dot{u}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Bigg)=\infty,

or

\limsup_{t\to T^{*}}\Bigg(\left\lVert\partial D(t)\right\rVert_{\text{Lip}}+\left\lvert\partial D(t)\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\frac{1}{\left\lvert\partial D(t)\right\rvert_{\text{inf}}}\Bigg)=\infty,

or

\limsup_{t\to T^{*}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\inf_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mu(\rho(t),c(t))}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu(\rho(t),c(t))\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\partial D(t))}\Bigg)\geqslant 10\varepsilon_{1},

or

\limsup_{t\to T^{*}}\Psi\big(\partial D(t),\mu(\rho(t),c(t)),\nu(\rho(t),c(t))\big)\geqslant 10\varepsilon_{2}.

As pointed out in Remark 1.2 of
[
44
]
, the smallness assumption of the shear viscosity fluctuation
is imposed to derive an estimate for the
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-norm (with
p\in(2,16]
) for the gradient
of the solution
v=v(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
of the following (stationary) elliptic equation:

(A.3)

-\dvg\big(2\mu\mathbb{D}v+\lambda\dvg vI_{3}\big)=\dvg G.

Here
\mu=\mu(x);\lambda=\lambda(x)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and
G=\big(G^{jk}(x)\big)_{1\leqslant j,k\leqslant 3}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
are given. The argument was to perturb the shear viscosity around the constant state
\widetilde{\mu}

-\widetilde{\mu}\Delta v-\nabla\big((\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda)\dvg v\big)=\dvg G+\dvg\big(2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v\big).

From this we deduce

(A.4)

\begin{cases}-\widetilde{\mu}\Delta\mathbb{P}v=\dvg\mathbb{P}G+\dvg\mathbb{P}\big(2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v\big),\\
-\Delta\big((2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda)\dvg v\big)=\dvg\dvg G+\dvg\dvg\big(2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v\big),\end{cases}

and hence

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}\nabla v

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mu}\nabla\mathbb{P}v+\widetilde{\mu}\nabla\big(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P}\big)v

\displaystyle=\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P}G-\widetilde{\mu}\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\dvg v+\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P}\big(2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v\big)

\displaystyle=\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P}G-\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\Big(\frac{\widetilde{\mu}}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg G\Big)

(A.5)

\displaystyle+\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P}\big(2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v\big)-\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\Big(\frac{\widetilde{\mu}}{2\widetilde{\mu}+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg\big(2(\mu-\widetilde{\mu})\mathbb{D}v\big)\Big).

Since the linear operators
\partial_{j}(-\Delta)^{-1}\partial_{k}

are continuous on
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, with
p\in(1,\infty)
, it follows that there exists a constant
C(p)>0
such that

\widetilde{\mu}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C(p)\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C(p)\left\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Then we assume that the shear viscosity fluctuation is small

(A.6)

C(p)\left\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}<\widetilde{\mu},

in order to absorb the last term on the LHS, and whence derive an estimate for the
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-norm of
\nabla v
. By a similar (yet more technical) perturbation argument, we derived piecewise Hölder bound for the velocity gradient.

To pass from the smallness condition of the viscosity fluctuation to that of its jump across the discontinuity surface, we first derive the following expression from
\eqref{ap:eq4}_{1}
(see the computations that led to (
2.6
)):

\displaystyle\nabla v

\displaystyle=\nabla\mathbb{P}v-\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\dvg v

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu}\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P}G-\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\bigg(\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg G\bigg)

(A.7)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\mu}\big[\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P},\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)-\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\bigg(\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\bigg).

We estimate the
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and piecewise Hölder norms of the last two terms above using the following lemma, whose proof is deferred to the end of this section.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\mathcal{T}
be a singular integral operator of Calderón-Zygmung type of even-order, associated with a homogeneous kernel
K
. Assume that the assumptions and notations in Definition
1.1
hold for
\Sigma
.
Let
p\in(1,\infty)
,
w,g\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and
g\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

(1)

L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-
estimate
. Let
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha]\cap(0,\frac{3}{p^{\prime}})
(
p^{\prime}
denotes the Hölder conjugate exponent of
p
), and assume that
g\in L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, with
\frac{1}{r}=\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{3}+\frac{1}{p}<1
. We have

(A.8)

\left\lVert\big[\mathcal{T},w\big]g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C\left\lvert w\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\big(\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big).

(2)

L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-
estimate
. Let
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha]
,
r\in(\frac{3}{\alpha^{\prime}},\infty]
, and assume that
g\in L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. We have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\big[\mathcal{T},w\big]g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Big(\left\lvert w\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big)\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(A.9)

\displaystyle+C\Big(\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell_{\Sigma}^{-\alpha^{\prime}}\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big).

(3)

\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\beta}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-
estimate
. Let
\beta\in(0,\alpha]
and
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\min\{\alpha,\beta\}]
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\big[\mathcal{T},w\big]g\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\beta}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Big(\left\lvert w\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\beta}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\big(\ell^{-\beta}_{\Sigma}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma}\left\lvert\Sigma\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\beta}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big)\left\lVert\mathcal{T}g,g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(A.10)

\displaystyle+C\Big(\left\lvert w\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\beta-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\beta+\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big)\left\lvert g\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\left\lvert g\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\beta}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Above,
\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma}
is a polynomial in
\left\lvert\Sigma\right\rvert
,
\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\text{Lip}}
, and
\left\lvert\Sigma\right\rvert_{\text{inf}}^{-1}
.

The constant
C
is independent of
g
,
w
and
\Sigma
.

The proof of Lemma
A.2
combines, in a nontrivial manner, the extension theorem for piecewise Hölder regular functions (see
[
24
]
), and the continuity of Calderón-Zygmung operators on
L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, with
q\in(1,\infty)
(see
[
16
,
17
]
).

We now apply it to the last terms of (
A.7
).

Estimate of the
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
norm

We consider
p\in(2,\infty)
, and
r\in[2,p)
such that
\frac{1}{r}=\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{3}+\frac{1}{p}
, for some
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha]
.
By applying (
A.8
) to the last terms in (
A.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{\mu}\big[\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P},\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big);\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\bigg(\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\bigg)\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(A.11)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C_{*}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big),

and it follows that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(A.12)

\displaystyle+C_{*}\bigg(\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\bigg).

Let us fix an arbitrary
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and assume that

(A.13)

\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\leqslant\varepsilon.

The first term on the RHS of (
A
) can then be absorbed into the LHS, yielding:

\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant\frac{C_{*}}{1-\varepsilon}\bigg(\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\bigg).

An energy estimate on (
A.3
) yields

\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C_{*}\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

and interpolation inequality gives

\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C\left\lVert\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{1-\theta}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\theta}\\
\leqslant C_{*}\left\lVert\nabla u\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{1-\theta}\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\theta},\text{ with}\quad\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}=\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{3\theta}.

Finally, applying Young’s inequality, we obtain:

(A.14)

\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant\frac{C_{*}}{1-\varepsilon}\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C_{*}(1-\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{\theta}}\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\theta}}.

We emphasize that only the smallness of the shear viscosity jump is required (see (
A.13
)), rather than that of the
overall shear viscosity fluctuation (see (
A.6
)). We now turn to deriving an estimate for the piecewise Hölder norm of
\nabla v
.

Estimate of the
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
norm

We fix some
\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha)
and
r\in(3/\alpha^{\prime},\infty)
. First, we apply (
2
) and (
3
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\big[\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P},\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(A.15)

\displaystyle+C\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

and

\displaystyle\left\lvert\big[\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P},\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\big(\ell^{-\alpha}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)\left\lVert\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P}\mathbb{D}v,\,\mathbb{D}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(A.16)

\displaystyle+C\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Next, we estimate the second-to-last term in (
A.7
) as follows:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{1}{\mu}\big[\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P},\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\dfrac{1}{\mu_{*}^{2}}\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert\big[\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P},\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\big[\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P},\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\dfrac{C}{\mu_{*}^{2}}\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\nabla u,\,\nabla\mathbb{P}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big(1+\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)+\big(\ell^{-\alpha}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big(\ell^{-\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)\Bigg]

(A.17)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Applying (
A.45
), the last term in (
A.7
) can be estimated as

\displaystyle\left\lvert\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\bigg(\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\bigg)\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert 2\mu+\lambda\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}\Big)\left\lVert\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big](2\mathbb{D}v)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

and hence (similar estimates to (
A.15
)-(
A.16
) also hold for
[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}](2\mathbb{D}v)
)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\bigg(\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\bigg)\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}\Big)\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\ell^{-\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\Bigg]

(A.18)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Combining (
A.7
), (
A.17
), and (
A.18
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lvert\nabla u_{G}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\mu,\,\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}\Big)\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\nabla v,\,\nabla\mathbb{P}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\mu,\,\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\mu,\,\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\ell^{-\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Hereafter,
\nabla u_{G}
denotes

\nabla u_{G}=\frac{1}{\mu}\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\mathbb{P}G-\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\bigg(\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg G\bigg).

The smallness of the shear viscosity jump (see (
A.13
)) helps absorb the last term above into the LHS.
Next, we use the interpolation inequality

(A.19)

\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant 2\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3}}^{1-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\alpha}}\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}^{\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\alpha}}

and Young’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{1-\varepsilon}\left\lvert\nabla u_{G}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\Psi_{\varepsilon}\left\lVert\nabla v,\,\nabla\mathbb{P}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{C}{\mu_{*}(1-\varepsilon)}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\mu,\,\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}\Big)\Big(\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lVert\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big),

where,

\displaystyle\Psi_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mu_{*}(1-\varepsilon)}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\mu,\,\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\ell^{-\alpha}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\big(\mu_{*}(1-\varepsilon)\big)^{\alpha/(\alpha-\alpha^{\prime})}}\Bigg[\left\lvert\mu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\mu,\,\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}+\ell^{-\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\Bigg]^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}}.

For the reader convenience, we shall denotes by
C_{**}
a generic constant that depends on the piecewise regularity of
\mu
,
\lambda
, as well
\ell_{\Sigma_{0}}
,
\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}
,
\big|\Sigma_{0}\big|_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha}}
. For instance the above estimate can be written as

(A.20)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\nabla v\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant\frac{C}{1-\varepsilon}\left\lvert\nabla u_{G}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{C_{**}}{1-\varepsilon}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\Psi_{\varepsilon}\left\lVert\nabla v,\,\nabla\mathbb{P}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

We will now derive an estimate for the
L^{\infty}
-norm of
\nabla v,\nabla\mathbb{P}v
.

Observe that (
A.15
) already provides a bound for the second term in the expression (
A.7
). We express the last term as

\displaystyle-\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\bigg(\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\Big[\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla,\,\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}\Big]\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)

(A.21)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\bigg(\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\bigg),

and applying (
2
)-(
A
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Big[\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla,\,\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}\Big]\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\Big(\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\left\lvert\nu\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}\Big)\left\lVert\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}^{2}}\left\lVert\llbracket\nu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lVert\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{**}\Big(\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{3/(\alpha-\alpha^{\prime})}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}^{2}}\left\lVert\llbracket\nu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}^{2}}\left\lVert\llbracket\nu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{0})}+\big(\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha^{\prime}}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Using (
A.44
)-(
2
)-(
A
) we can estimate the last term of (
A.21
) as follows

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{1}{2\mu+\lambda}\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla\bigg(\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\bigg)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\ell_{\Sigma_{0}}^{-\frac{3}{r}}\left\lVert\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\dvg\dvg,\mu-\widetilde{\mu}\big]\big(2\mathbb{D}v\big)\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{**}\Big(\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{3/(\alpha-\alpha^{\prime})}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{0})}+\big(\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha^{\prime}}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

By combining all these computations, we obtain the following estimate (we freely use
\frac{1}{\mu_{*}}\lVert\llbracket\nu\rrbracket\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\leqslant C
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\lVert\nabla u_{G}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C_{**}\Big(\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{3/(\alpha-\alpha^{\prime})}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{0})}+\big(\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha^{\prime}}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Applying the Leray projector
\mathbb{P}
to (
A.27
) and repeating the above computations, we observe that a similar estimate also holds for
\nabla\mathbb{P}
. Next, (
A.19
) and Young’s inequality yield

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla v,\,\nabla\mathbb{P}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lVert\nabla u_{G},\,\mathbb{P}\nabla u_{G}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C_{**}\Big(\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{3/(\alpha-\alpha^{\prime})}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)+\Big(\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}}\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{0})}+\big(\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha^{\prime}}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

and (
A.20
) leads to

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla v,\,\nabla\mathbb{P}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{**}(\varepsilon)\Big(\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{3/(\alpha-\alpha^{\prime})}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\left\lVert\nabla u_{G},\mathbb{P}\nabla u_{G}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}}\left\lvert\nabla u_{G}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(A.22)

\displaystyle+\Bigg[\Psi_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}}+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{0})}+\big(\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha^{\prime}}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)\Bigg]\left\lVert\nabla v,\nabla\mathbb{P}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Assuming that

(A.23)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha^{\prime}}}+\frac{C}{\mu_{*}}\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma_{0})}+\big(\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma_{0}}+\mathfrak{P}_{\Sigma_{0}}\left\lvert\Sigma_{0}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{1+\alpha^{\prime}}}\big)\left\lVert\llbracket\mu\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{0})}\Big)<\varepsilon^{\prime}<1,

the last term can be absorbed in the LHS and interpolation and Young’s inequalities imply:

(A.24)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla v,\,\nabla\mathbb{P}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C_{**}(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})\Big(\left\lVert\nabla u_{G},\mathbb{P}\nabla u_{G}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lvert\nabla u_{G}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big).

Upon inserting this estimate into (
A.20
), we obtain

(A.25)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla v,\,\nabla\mathbb{P}v\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C_{**}(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})\left\lVert\nabla v\right\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C(\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime})\Big(\left\lVert\nabla u_{G},\mathbb{P}\nabla u_{G}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\left\lvert\nabla u_{G}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big).

The above two estimate with
\dvg G=\dvg F-\rho\dot{u}
, will replace estimate (3.11) in
[
44
]
. We shall use (
A.14
)
to derive an estimate for the
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-norm of
\nabla u
and
\nabla\dot{u}
, and hence close the energy estimates for
\dot{u}
and
\ddot{u}
. Here, we only rely on (
A.13
)–(
A.23
), which is significantly more favorable than the smallness condition (3.5) in
[
44
]
, in the sense that only the smallness of the viscosities jump is required, not that of the full fluctuation of the viscosities. The remainder of the proof proceeds identically.

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

To prove Lemma
A.2
, we first observe that since
w\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, there exists a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-regular extension
w^{e}
of
w
that satisfies the following properties:

(A.26)

\displaystyle\left\lVert w^{e}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\quad w^{e}=w\text{ on }D^{c},

(A.27)

\displaystyle\left\lVert w-w^{e}\right\rVert_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\ell^{\frac{1}{q}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{q}(\Sigma)}\text{ for all }q\in[1,\infty],

(A.28)

\displaystyle\left\lvert w-w^{e}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\beta}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lvert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\beta}(\Sigma)}+C\ell^{-\beta}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)},\quad\beta\in(0,\alpha],

(A.29)

\displaystyle\left\lvert w^{e}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lvert w\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\beta}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\ell^{-\beta}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)},\quad\beta\in(0,\alpha].

The above extension result is an updated version of Lemma A.2 in
[
44
]
, originally established in
[
24
, Lemma 5.2]
for the two-dimensional case.

Proof of (
A.8
)

Our approach consists in substituting
w
with
w^{e}+(w-w^{e})
, which yields

(A.30)

\big[\mathcal{T},w\big]g=\big[\mathcal{T},w^{e}\big]g+\big[\mathcal{T},(w-w^{e})\big]g.

Given that
w^{e}
is
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-regular (in the whole space), the first term is now more
regular
than the LHS. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\big[\mathcal{T},w^{e}\big]g(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}K(x-y)\big(w^{e}(y)-w^{e}(x)\big)g(y)dy\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lvert w^{e}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\big(\left\lvert\cdot\right\rvert^{-3+\alpha^{\prime}}\ast\left\lvert g\right\rvert\big)(x),\text{ for all }\alpha^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha].

For
0<\alpha^{\prime}<\frac{3}{p^{\prime}}
, we can apply Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev’s inequality (see
[
1
, Theorem 1.7]
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\big[\mathcal{T},w^{e}\big]g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lvert w^{e}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\text{ recall }\frac{1}{r}=\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{3}+\frac{1}{p}<1

(A.31)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\big(\left\lvert w\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\big)\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\text{ recall }\eqref{ap:eq9}.

To estimate the
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-norm of the last term of (
A.30
), we rely on the boundedness of commutators between
Calderón–Zygmund operators and
BMO(\mathbb{R}^{3})
functions on
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\big[\mathcal{T},(w-w^{e})\big]g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lVert w-w^{e}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(A.32)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\quad\text{recall }\eqref{ap:eq5}.

Estimate (
A.8
) follows directly from (
A.31
)–(
A.32
). We now proceed to the proof of (
2
).

Proof of (
2
)

We recall that the first term of the expression (
A.30
) reads:

\displaystyle\big(\big[\mathcal{T},w^{e}\big]g\big)(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}K(x-z)\big(w^{e}(z)-w^{e}(x)\big)g(z)dz

\displaystyle=\int_{B(x,1)}K(x-z)\big(w^{e}(z)-w^{e}(x)\big)g(z)dz+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus B(x,1)}K(x-z)\big(w^{e}(z)-w^{e}(x)\big)g(z)dz.

To estimate the first term, we use the
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
regularity of
w^{e}
to mitigate the singularity of the kernel
K
:

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{B(x,1)}K(x-z)

\displaystyle\big(w^{e}(z)-w^{e}(x)\big)g(z)dz\Bigg|\leqslant C\left\lvert w^{e}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\int_{B(x,1)}\left\lvert x-z\right\rvert^{-3+\alpha^{\prime}}\left\lvert g(z)\right\rvert dz

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lvert w^{e}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{1}\tau^{(-3+\alpha^{\prime})r^{\prime}+2})d\tau\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{r^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lvert w^{e}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\quad\text{recall that }\frac{1}{r}<\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{3},

\displaystyle\leqslant C\big(\left\lvert w\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\big)\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\text{ recall }\eqref{ap:eq9}.

Using the decay of the kernel
K
, we derive:

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus B(x,1)}K(x-z)

\displaystyle\big(w^{e}(z)-w^{e}(x)\big)g(z)dz\bigg|\leqslant C\left\lVert w^{e}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus B(x,1)}\left\lvert x-z\right\rvert^{-3}\left\lvert g(z)\right\rvert dz

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lVert w^{e}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Bigg(\int_{1}^{\infty}\tau^{-3p^{\prime}+2}d\tau\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lVert w^{e}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\quad\text{recall that }p^{\prime}>1,

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\quad\text{recall }\eqref{ap:eq11}.

It follows that

(A.33)

\left\lVert\big[\mathcal{T},w^{e}\big]g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leqslant C\Big(\left\lvert w\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big)\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

To estimate the
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
-norm of the last term of (
A.30
), we use the fact that
(w-w^{e})\mathbb{1}_{D^{c}}=0

to express:

\diamond

for
x\in D
,

(A.34)

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{T},(w-w^{e})\big]g(x)

\displaystyle=\big[\mathcal{T},(w-w^{e})\big]\big(g\mathbb{1}_{D}\big)(x)-(w-w^{e})(x)\mathcal{T}\big(g\mathbb{1}_{D^{c}}\big)(x).

\diamond

for
x\in D^{c}
,

(A.35)

\big[\mathcal{T},(w-w^{e})\big]g(x)=\mathcal{T}\big((w-w^{e})g\mathbb{1}_{D}\big)(x).

Since
(w-w^{e})\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\overline{D})
, we follow the computations leading to (
A.33
) to estimate the first term in the expression (
A.34
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\big[\mathcal{T},(w-w^{e})\big]\big(g\mathbb{1}_{D}\big)\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(D)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Big(\left\lvert w-w^{e}\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\overline{D})}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w-w^{e}\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big)\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(D)}+C\left\lVert w-w^{e}\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(D)}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(D)}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big)\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+C\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\text{ recall }\eqref{ap:eq5}-\eqref{ap:eq15}.

To estimate the last term of (
A.34
) we express:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\big(g\mathbb{1}_{D^{c}}\big)(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{D^{c}\cap B(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)\big(g(y)-g(\check{x})\big)dy+g(\check{x})\int_{D^{c}\cap B(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{D^{c}\cap B^{c}(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)g(y)dy,

where
\check{x}\in\partial D
such that
d(x,D^{c})=\left\lvert x-\check{x}\right\rvert
. It follows that

\Bigg|\int_{D^{c}\cap B(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)\big(g(y)-g(\check{x})\big)dy\Bigg|\leqslant C\left\lvert g\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

and

\Bigg|\int_{D^{c}\cap B^{c}(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)g(y)dy\Bigg|\leqslant C\ell_{\Sigma}^{-\frac{3}{p}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

Using the extra-cancellation of even-order Riesz kernels (
[
2
,
36
]
), we bound the remaining term as

\Bigg|g(\check{x})\int_{D^{c}\cap B(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)dy\Bigg|\leqslant C\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

We thus obtain the following bound for the last term of (
A.34
):

(A.36)

\left\lvert(w-w^{e})(x)\mathcal{T}\big(g\mathbb{1}_{D^{c}}\big)(x)\right\rvert\leqslant C\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big(\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell_{\Sigma}^{-\frac{3}{p}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big).

For (
A.35
), we consider
\check{x}\in\partial D
such that
d(x,D)=\left\lvert x-\check{x}\right\rvert
and we express:

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{T},(w-w^{e})\big]g(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{D\cap B(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)\big((w-w^{e})(y)-(w-w^{e})(\check{x})g(y)dy

\displaystyle+(w-w^{e})(\check{x})\int_{D\cap B(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)g(y)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{D\cap B^{c}(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)(w-w^{e})(y)g(y)dy.

Estimate (
A.36
) also applies to the last two terms above, whereas the first one can be bounded as follows:

\Bigg|\int_{D\cap B(\check{x},\ell_{\Sigma})}K(x-y)\big((w-w^{e})(y)-(w-w^{e})(\check{x})g(y)dy\Bigg|\leqslant C\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big)\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}.

In sum, we obtain the following bound for the last term of (
A.30
) (recall that
\frac{3}{r}<\alpha^{\prime}
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\big[\mathcal{T},(w-w^{e})\big]g\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Big(\left\lvert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rvert_{\dot{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\ell^{-\alpha^{\prime}}_{\Sigma}\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big)\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

(A.37)

\displaystyle+C\left\lVert\llbracket w\rrbracket\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}\Big(\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha^{\prime}}_{\text{pw},\Sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}+\ell_{\Sigma}^{-\alpha^{\prime}}\left\lVert g\right\rVert_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\Big).

Finally, (
2
) follows directly from (
A.33
)–(
A.37
). We now move on to the proof of (
3
).

Proof of (
3
)

Let
x,y
be two points located on the same side of
\Sigma
and let us assume
\delta=\left\lvert x-y\right\rvert\in(0,\ell_{\Sigma}/2)
. Without lost of generality, assume that
x,y\in\overline{D}
.
By taking the difference of the first term in expression (
A.30
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\big(\big[\mathcal{T},w^{e}\big]g\big)(x)

\displaystyle-\big(\big[\mathcal{T},w^{e}\big]g\big)(y)=\int_{B(y,2\delta)}K(x-z)\big(w^{e}(z)-w^{e}(x)\big)g(z)dz

\displaystyle-\int_{B(y,2\delta)}K(y-z)\big(w^{e}(z)-w^{e}(y)\big)g(z)dz

\displaystyle+\big(w^{e}(y)-w^{e}(x)\big)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus B(y,2\delta)}K(x-z)g(z)dz

(A.38)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus B(y,2\delta)}\big(K(x-z)-K(y-z)\big)\big(w^{e}(z)-w^{e}(y)\big)g(z)dz.

One derives easily the following bound for the first two terms of the expression above:
